- **cumulant-order-block-sparse-attention**: COBS - block sparse attention selector that stores a compressed SECOND-order (cumulant) statistic per block instead of only first-order (attention mass), closing the gap to dense attention at ~15x less KV-cache read traffic. Use when building/improving block-sparse attention (NSA-style) for long-context LLMs and existing selectors under-select. ([hiyenwong/ai_collection](https://github.com/hiyenwong/ai_collection/tree/HEAD/collection/skills/other/cumulant-order-block-sparse-attention) · ★4)
- **cumulative-abundance-rank-thresholding**: Use when many clustered gene representatives (e.g., 60 amoA-AOB clusters from 96% CD-HIT clustering) must be prioritized by environmental abundance/prevalence so only a minimal, high-coverage subset is carried forward to primer design. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cumulative-abundance-rank-thresholding))
- **cumulative-classification-fraction-visualization**: Use when benchmarking a read-until classifier against sequence-based adaptive sampling and you need a speed comparison table (riser_vs_as_timing.csv) and a cumulative-classified-vs-time plot (speed_comparison.png). ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cumulative-classification-fraction-visualization))
- **cumulative-cohort-dose-escalation**: Use when implementing or reproducing a phase I/II immunotherapy trial (e.g., the TSNP design) and you need to conduct the first-stage toxicity exploration — escalating, de-escalating, or retaining doses in cohorts of m patients based on isotonic toxicity estimates versus φ−τ. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cumulative-cohort-dose-escalation))
- **cumulative-contribution-abundance-computation**: Use when a MAF (or VCF) somatic mutation dataset is paired with known signature matrices P (e.g., COSMIC v3.2 SBS) and S, and you need per-gene signature-gene association (CCA) tables for downstream filtering or visualization. - **cumulative-coverage-feature-timeline-analysis**: Use when streaming nanopore reads in npScarf real-time mode with -resistGene/-oriRep annotation databases and you need to know how many Mb (or fold coverage) of long reads were required before each AMR gene or plasmid origin was reported. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-genomics/tree/HEAD/collections/genomics/v1/leaves/cumulative-coverage-feature-timeline-analysis))
- **cumulative-distribution-analysis**: Use when comparing two sample groups' circRNA expression distributions quantified per-circRNA (e.g. FPBcirc or CIRCscore from CLEAR quant.txt outputs) to decide whether one metric better discriminates the groups. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cumulative-distribution-analysis))

([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cumulative-distribution-calculation))
- **cumulative-distribution-function-matching**: Use when you have an nx1 vector of pseudotime values from a trajectory-inference algorithm (Wanderlust, Diffusion Maps/DPT, Monocle) and independent prior knowledge of the cell density distribution on the desired real scale, and you need to convert pseudotime to true time or distance-from-surface. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/cumulative-distribution-function-matching))
- **cumulative-distribution-visualization**: Use when when you have per-feature quality metrics (such as CV values from NMR or MS reproducibility analysis) and need to: (1) confirm that a specified proportion of features meet regulatory thresholds (e.g., 99% &lt; 0.30, 92% &lt; 0.15 for CV); ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v2/leaves/cumulative-distribution-visualization))
- **cumulative-frequency-distribution-plotting**: Use when comparing the precision of corrected vs raw LC-MS/GC-MS metabolomics or proteomics feature tables by measuring how many features fall below successive RSD (CV%) thresholds in QC samples. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/cumulative-frequency-distribution-plotting))
- **cumulative-identity-distribution-analysis**: Use when benchmarking sequence clustering tools (Linclust, MMseqs2, CD-HIT, UCLUST) on huge sets and you need to quantify clustering quality (false negatives) without exhaustively comparing all pairs. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metagenomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metagenomics/v1/leaves/cumulative-identity-distribution-analysis))
- **cumulative-layout-shift**: Use when auditing slow page loads, heavy assets, or rendering delays related to Minimize cumulative layout shift. ([coastdigitalgroup/coastai-skills](https://github.com/coastdigitalgroup/coastai-skills/tree/HEAD/website-development/cumulative-layout-shift-mitigation) · ★2)
- **cumulative-note-synthesis**: Synthesizing a set of source documents (survey responses, reports, competitor material, papers, interview notes, tickets) by reading them one at a time into a single cumulative note, auditing that note at fixed intervals for absence, imprecision, contradiction and fragility, and carrying the audit findings forward as explicit focus directives for the next document. Use whenever more than one source must be analyzed toward one shared question, instead of asking the model about each source in isolation. - **cumulative-regret**: Evaluates piecewise-stationary multi-armed bandit algorithms by measuring the expected cumulative regret over a sequence of time steps. It probes how well an algorithm adapts to changing arm reward distributions (change-points) while balancing exploration and exploitation. Use when the user has predictions and gold and needs to compute cumulative regret. ([qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool](https://github.com/qhjqhj00/research-skills-pool/tree/HEAD/skill-factory/output/cumulative-regret) · ★3)

- **cunninghams-law**: Apply Cunningham's Law when someone wants to elicit information, feedback, or corrections from others—especially online. Trigger on phrases like "how do I get people to respond?", "nobody is answering my question", "I want to get feedback on this", or when someone is drafting a question for Stack Overflow, a forum, a team Slack, or a pull request. Also useful when discussing how to get a conversation started or surface hidden knowledge within a team.
